- [BINP — publications](https://binp.kaist.ac.kr/publications) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2026 and historical entries read; July 10 CMOS microcoil paper used only as bibliographic evidence.

### Projects

#### Implantable microcoil foundation, 2016

*Preclinical landmark study*

The primary study demonstrated cortical-neuron activation in slices and behavioral responses in animals. The reported narrow activation region was measured in a slice experiment; it must not be presented as proven human visual resolution or lifelong implant stability. [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5148213/)

#### Integrated micromagnetic system, 2026

*Published circuit work; result details unreviewed*

The current lab bibliography lists a July paper on a substrate-aware CMOS stimulation system with a bent microcoil and programmable triangular-current driver. The listed architecture supports a recent circuit direction; biological performance was not verified because the publisher page was inaccessible. [3](https://binp.kaist.ac.kr/publications)

### Progress to track: Activation selectivity and engineering constraints

Compare recruitment geometry, power, heating and stable responses under specified conditions. Magnetic stimulation can still require a surgically implanted device. [1](https://bcs.kaist.ac.kr/sub020101/view/id/116) [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5148213/)

### Study context

The KAIST faculty page confirms Lee joined in 2023 after work at MGH/Harvard. The pre-2023 papers are earlier work by the investigator, not experiments originally performed in the new KAIST laboratory. Human treatment efficacy is not established by these sources. [1](https://bcs.kaist.ac.kr/sub020101/view/id/116) [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5148213/)

### Editorial notebook question

Editorial question: Can a compact magnetic interface preserve spatial selectivity after packaging, implantation, and changes in surrounding tissue?

## CashLab — Human Cortical Physiology Laboratory

**Massachusetts General Hospital / Harvard Medical School · United States**

Investigators: Sydney Cash

Areas: Neural interfaces; Computational neuroscience; Neural decoding

Sydney Cash’s MGH/Harvard laboratory studies human cortical activity during epilepsy and tumor surgery, linking basic physiology to neurotechnology. It combines clinical electrodes with research probes to examine oscillations, cognition and transitions into pathological activity. [1](https://pinphd.hms.harvard.edu/people/sydney-cash) [4](https://www.massgeneralbrigham.org/en/doctors/c/sydney-cash-3007848)

High-density recordings and signal processing resolve single-unit activity and local field potentials at different spatial scales. Much of this evidence comes from short intraoperative research sessions, not chronic home-use BCI systems. [1](https://pinphd.hms.harvard.edu/people/sydney-cash) [4](https://www.massgeneralbrigham.org/en/doctors/c/sydney-cash-3007848)

**Methods:** Intraoperative electrophysiology; Neuropixels recording; Clinical/research electrode comparison; Single-unit sorting; Signal processing.

**Signals:** Single-unit spikes; Local field potentials; Epileptiform activity.

Publisher abstract and date read; over 200 isolated units during intraoperative recording.
- [High-Density Multi-Depth Human Recordings Using 45 mm Long Neuropixels Probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09912) — Abstract / publication record read; checked 2026-09-15. Preprint abstract, author affiliations and date read; nine patients, acute workflow and reported events.
- [Mass General Brigham — Sydney Cash](https://www.massgeneralbrigham.org/en/doctors/c/sydney-cash-3007848) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Professional biography read; current MGH staff and Harvard professor affiliations. Patient comments are not research evidence.
- [CashLab homepage](https://cashlab.mgh.harvard.edu/) — Page unavailable; checked 2026-09-15. Lab server could not be retrieved; readable Harvard profile supplied current program information instead.

### Projects

#### Long Neuropixels probes in humans, 2026 preprint

*Human intraoperative preprint*

A January preprint reports intraoperative use of 45-mm Neuropixels probes in nine patients. Recordings sampled surface and deeper targets, including hippocampus and cingulate cortex; the authors report no probe breakage or adverse events in this cohort. This remains a small feasibility study and preprint. [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09912)

#### Human cortical single units, 2022

*Human intraoperative study*

The published study describes recording over 200 well-isolated cortical single units with Neuropixels during surgery and analyzing waveform diversity and relationships to field-potential events. That is a sorted-unit result in the reported recordings, not the probe’s electrode count. [2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-021-00997-0)

### Progress to track: Usable units, anatomical depth and recording conditions

The 45-mm dimension describes probe length; nine is the 2026 cohort size; over 200 refers to isolated units in the 2022 work. These denominators cannot be pooled as one channel-growth series. [2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-021-00997-0) [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09912)

### Study context

Research is performed in the context of clinical neurosurgery, with epilepsy and cortical physiology as major interests. Acute feasibility and reported absence of events do not establish chronic implant safety or a marketed device. [1](https://pinphd.hms.harvard.edu/people/sydney-cash) [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09912)

### Editorial notebook question

Editorial question: Which cellular events reliably explain a clinically useful large-scale signal, and how much does that relationship change with brain state or pathology?

## Bioelectronics Group — Anikeeva Laboratory

**Massachusetts Institute of Technology · United States**

Investigators: Polina Anikeeva

Areas: Bioelectronics; Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation

Polina Anikeeva’s MIT Bioelectronics Group develops materials and devices to study neural circuits and communication between the brain and peripheral organs. Its tools include multifunctional fibers, magnetic nanomaterials and newer molecular-delivery platforms. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/polina-anikeeva/) [5](https://bioelectronics.mit.edu/)

The group combines materials synthesis and microelectronics with neurobiology. Flexible fibers can deliver light or fluid and record signals; injected magnetic particles convert externally applied fields into local stimulation. Wireless actuation does not necessarily mean no invasive delivery step. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/polina-anikeeva/) [5](https://bioelectronics.mit.edu/)

**Methods:** Thermally drawn fibers; Multifunctional microelectronics; Magnetic nanomaterials; Optogenetics; Microfluidic delivery.

**Signals:** Electrical neural activity; Optical stimulation; Temperature; Organ-linked behavior.

### Projects

#### Magnetoelectric nanodiscs, 2024

*Cell culture and mouse experiments*

The institutional report describes magnetic-core/piezoelectric-shell particles tested in cultured neurons and injected into mouse brain regions. Magnetic fields modulated activity and behavior without a transgene requirement. Injection remains invasive, and clinical use requires additional safety and efficacy work. [2](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2024/10/11/tiny-magnetic-discs-offer-remote-brain-stimulation-without-transgenes/)

#### Microelectronic fibers for gut–brain circuits, 2023

*Preclinical mouse platform*

Thermally drawn fibers integrate electrodes, light sources, temperature sensors and microfluidics. The primary paper reports wireless experiments in mice, including intestinal manipulation of circuits associated with feeding and reward. Continuous fiber manufacture is not equivalent to the number of simultaneously recorded neurons. [3](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41587-023-01833-5)

#### CHARIOT-AAV, 2026 preprint

*Preclinical molecular-platform preprint*

A coauthored preprint proposes conjugating delivery vectors to AAVs to combine targeting with larger payloads. It reports molecular-delivery experiments, including brain-directed applications; this is a separate gene-delivery direction, not evidence of an improved electrode implant. [4](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.04.13.718054v1)

### Progress to track: Modalities, targeting and tissue compatibility

Useful comparisons include which functions share a fiber, controllable dose or optical delivery, signal quality and biological response. Particle size, fiber length and channel count describe different things. [2](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2024/10/11/tiny-magnetic-discs-offer-remote-brain-stimulation-without-transgenes/) [3](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41587-023-01833-5)

### Study context

The group’s materials enable neuroscience experiments. The magnetic-particle report explicitly identifies further large-animal safety work before clinical translation. Anikeeva’s current institutional profile confirms MIT materials-science department leadership and the Brain–Body Center. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/polina-anikeeva/) [2](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2024/10/11/tiny-magnetic-discs-offer-remote-brain-stimulation-without-transgenes/)

### Editorial notebook question

Editorial question: Which combination of optical, electrical, and chemical access is necessary to identify a causal gut–brain pathway without changing the behavior being studied?

## Synthetic Neurobiology Group — Boyden Laboratory

**Massachusetts Institute of Technology · United States**

Investigators: Edward Boyden

Areas: Neuroimaging; Bioelectronics; Neuromodulation; Computational neuroscience

Ed Boyden’s MIT Synthetic Neurobiology Group develops tools for observing and manipulating neural systems. Its research spans optogenetics, molecularly multiplexed microscopy and methods that make fast activity across many brain regions accessible. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/ed-boyden/) [4](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2026/08/14/high-speed-microscopy-reveals-electrical-activity-across-the-brain/)

The group combines engineered reporters with optical and chemical methods. Expansion microscopy examines molecular organization in processed tissue; voltage imaging observes fast activity in living genetically labeled neurons. These measure different biological properties. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/ed-boyden/) [4](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2026/08/14/high-speed-microscopy-reveals-electrical-activity-across-the-brain/)

**Methods:** Remote-scanning light-sheet microscopy; Genetically encoded voltage indicators; Expansion microscopy; Serial molecular labeling; Optogenetics.

**Signals:** Fluorescent voltage-indicator signals; Molecular protein labels.

### Website review

Rewritten from freshly read official pages and identified research evidence. All retained claim sources were read at the stated scope.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [Voltage imaging of neurons distributed across entire brains of larval zebrafish](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-026-03179-7) — 2026-08-14.

- Added August 2026 primary voltage-imaging study.
- Explicitly distinguished whole-brain spatial coverage from every-neuron signal yield.
- Separated fixed molecular maps from living electrical activity.

- [Voltage imaging of neurons distributed across entire brains of larval zebrafish](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-026-03179-7) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Publisher abstract and main introduction read; 200.8-Hz volumes, approximately one-quarter neuron coverage and larval zebrafish.
- [Multiplexed expansion revealing for imaging multiprotein nanostructures in healthy and diseased brain](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53729-w) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Abstract and introduction read; serial labeling, >20 proteins and mouse-tissue demonstrations.
- [High-speed microscopy reveals electrical activity across the brain](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/2026/08/14/high-speed-microscopy-reveals-electrical-activity-across-the-brain/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Research report read; method mechanism, usable signal fraction and future model-organism extensions.

### Projects

#### Brain-wide voltage imaging, 2026

*Living larval zebrafish imaging*

A remote-scanning light-sheet microscope measured voltage-indicator signals from neurons across larval zebrafish brains at 200.8 volumes per second. Approximately one-quarter of brain neurons yielded measured voltage traces. The result is brain-wide coverage, not simultaneous recording of every neuron and not a human brain scan. [2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-026-03179-7)

#### Multiplexed expansion revealing, 2024

*Fixed-tissue method study*

Repeated labeling and imaging visualized more than 20 proteins in the same expanded specimen. Demonstrations included 23 proteins in an Alzheimer’s-model mouse brain and 20 synaptic proteins in mouse cortex. Molecular maps of processed tissue are distinct from real-time electrical recordings. [3](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53729-w)

### Progress to track: Coverage, speed and molecular multiplexing

The 200.8-Hz value is a volumetric imaging rate; the approximate quarter-neuron yield describes usable reporter traces. Protein counts in expansion microscopy measure molecular multiplexing, not recording channels. [2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-026-03179-7) [3](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53729-w)

### Study context

The current MIT profile frames accurate brain simulation and repair as goals served by enabling tools. These experiments do not themselves demonstrate whole-brain emulation, a human clinical interface or repaired neurological disease. [1](https://mcgovern.mit.edu/profile/ed-boyden/) [2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41592-026-03179-7) [3](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53729-w)

### Editorial notebook question

Editorial question: How can increased molecular or temporal multiplexing preserve measurement accuracy and keep preparation and analysis practical for nonspecialist laboratories?

## neuroSPEED and MEG research program

**McGill University · Canada**

Investigators: Sylvain Baillet

Areas: Neuroimaging; Computational neuroscience

Develops analysis methods, computational models, and open research infrastructure for large-scale brain electrophysiology. [1](https://www.mcgill.ca/qls/researchers/sylvain-baillet) [2](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/meg-unit)

The program links fast electrophysiological measurements to models of brain systems, combining MEG analysis with software and shared datasets. McGill’s MEG unit describes neuroSPEED as its own research laboratory alongside its core-facility role. [1](https://www.mcgill.ca/qls/researchers/sylvain-baillet) [2](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/meg-unit)

### Projects

#### Brainstorm analysis tools

*Research software and methods*

The MEG unit contributes to Brainstorm, a collaborative open-source application for multimodal electrophysiology. Its practical role is to support analysis and interpretation of large datasets and make advanced workflows available to other researchers. [2](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/meg-unit)

#### Open MEG Archive

*Human research dataset, release 2025-07-17*

OMEGA combines MEG, anatomical MRI and participant information. Release 3.1.1 (17 July 2025) lists 644 participants. Its headline says 480 participants have co-registration, while the detailed status section says 479 first-session subjects; this unresolved discrepancy should be checked against the downloaded release. [3](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/neuroinformatics/omega)

### Progress to track: Reproducible coverage across participants

For this program, the scale question includes participant diversity, recording coverage, co-registration, and reusable analysis. Dataset participants and source-localized brain activity are not equivalent to implanted channels. [2](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/meg-unit) [3](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/neuroinformatics/omega)

### Study context

OMEGA requires controlled access and acceptance of its data-use agreement. The documentation reports migration to Nextcloud in December 2025. MEG source localization remains model-dependent; neither repository participants nor reconstructed sources are implanted recording channels. [1](https://www.mcgill.ca/qls/researchers/sylvain-baillet) [2](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/meg-unit) [3](https://www.mcgill.ca/bic/neuroinformatics/omega)

### Editorial notebook question

How robust are inferred network patterns when preprocessing, source models, and participant composition change?

## Monash Vision Group

**Monash University · Australia**

Investigators: Arthur Lowery; Marcello Rosa; Jeffrey Rosenfeld; Yan Wong

Areas: Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation; Bioelectronics; Rehabilitation & prosthetics

Monash Vision Group develops a cortical visual prosthesis that converts camera images into patterns of electrical stimulation. The program combines visual neuroscience, miniature wireless electronics, implant packaging, image processing, and neurosurgical expertise. [1](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye/technology) [3](https://www.monash.edu/news/articles/opening-eyes-to-a-frontier-in-vision-restoration)

Gennaris uses a camera and processor to turn scene information into stimulation commands for wireless cortical tiles. The named investigators are documented in Monash’s 2020 report; the current leadership roster could not be reread. Simulation experiments inform image processing but do not demonstrate vision restored in a human recipient. [1](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye/technology) [3](https://www.monash.edu/news/articles/opening-eyes-to-a-frontier-in-vision-restoration)

**Methods:** Camera-based feature extraction; Wireless power and data transmission; Intracortical electrical stimulation; Hermetic ceramic packaging; Phosphene-vision simulation.

**Signals:** Camera images; Electrical stimulation patterns; Simulated phosphene percepts; Behavioral task performance.

### Projects

#### Gennaris implant architecture

*Device architecture with preclinical evaluation*

The technology page specifies up to 11 tiles with 43 microelectrodes each, giving a first-generation design capacity of 473 electrodes. Each tile contains receiver and stimulation circuitry. This capacity is not a count verified in a current human recipient. [1](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye/technology) [3](https://www.monash.edu/news/articles/opening-eyes-to-a-frontier-in-vision-restoration)

#### Testing useful visual representations

*Simulation and design research*

An iPhone and VR simulation explores object pickup, locating moving people, and obstacle navigation. These are simulation results based on assumed phosphene appearance, distinct from outcomes in an implanted person. [1](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye/technology)

### Progress to track: 473-electrode design capacity

The figure is 11 × 43 across a proposed system. Perceptual quality also depends on electrode placement, activation selectivity, and the scene-processing strategy. [1](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye/technology)

### Study context

The live home page still describes a planned first-in-human study. The technology page documents device architecture and simulated phosphene tasks, not completed human efficacy or lifetime implant reliability. No dated 2024–2026 trial outcome was established in the pages inspected. [3](https://www.monash.edu/news/articles/opening-eyes-to-a-frontier-in-vision-restoration) [2](https://www.monash.edu/bioniceye)

### Editorial notebook question

Which visual features deliver the largest functional benefit when only a limited, irregular pattern of phosphenes is available?

## Interactive Applications for Neuro-Rehabilitation Laboratory (LANR)

**National Autonomous University of Mexico · Mexico**

Investigators: Ana María Escalante Gonzalbo; Yoás Ramírez Graullera

Areas: Rehabilitation & prosthetics

LANR’s official site describes interactive games and movement sensors for motor and cognitive rehabilitation at UNAM. The project pages document intended exercises and clinician-guided development; they do not report a controlled treatment effect. [1](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/) [2](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html) [3](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/colaboradores.html)

The site identifies Ana María Escalante Gonzalbo as academic coordinator and Yoás Ramírez Graullera as technical lead. Development uses Unity, Kinect, Leap Motion and custom interfaces. The pages are undated; their linked media items are from 2016–2019, so current recruitment and deployment are not inferred. [1](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/) [2](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html) [3](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/colaboradores.html)

**Methods:** Unity game development; Optical hand and body tracking; Custom data-glove interfaces; Clinician-guided task design; Movement-based rehabilitation assessment.

**Signals:** Hand and finger movement; Upper-limb range of motion; Gaze and neck movement; Game-task performance.

### Website review

Read the Spanish overview, complete game descriptions and named collaborator roles; searched explicitly for 2024–2026 updates without establishing a newer dated research result.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

No dated source established.

- Distinguished academic coordinator from technical lead.
- Labeled the undated site and older media timeline; retained goals rather than clinical efficacy claims.

- [LANR overview and development process](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Interdisciplinary mission, Unity and sensor workflow, hospital evaluation as development process; newest listed media date is 2019-08-07.
- [LANR games and rehabilitation objectives](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Charlie Escape, Penal Madness and other games; intended movements, not measured clinical benefits.
- [LANR collaborators and roles](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/colaboradores.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Named academic and technical roles and institutional collaborators.

### Projects

#### Charlie Escape: practicing hand opening

*Lab-developed rehabilitation application*

This Leap Motion game maps keeping the hand closed to forward movement and opening it to avoiding obstacles. The stated rehabilitation goals include grasping, pinch movements, and fine-motor control. The description establishes intended exercises rather than an effect size from a trial. [2](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html)

#### Penal Madness and targeted arm movement

*Lab-developed rehabilitation applications*

A Kinect-based goalkeeper game asks users to reach for shots and throw a ball with the hand and arm. Its listed targets include upper-limb flexion, extension, abduction, and adduction. Other games address finger pinch and visuomotor coordination. [2](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html)

### Progress to track: Movement quality and adherence

Appropriate metrics include range of motion, accurate repetitions, and sustained participation. The reviewed pages describe applications and intended benefits but provide no controlled clinical outcome figures. [1](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/) [2](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/juegos.html)

### Study context

The team reports clinician consultation and hospital evaluation as part of development. Home rehabilitation and remote monitoring are stated goals; they should be distinguished from demonstrated routine deployment. [1](https://lanr.ifc.unam.mx/)

### Editorial notebook question

Does better game performance reflect recovery of the intended movement, and does that improvement transfer to daily activities?

## NeuroAI Lab

**National University of Singapore · Singapore**

Investigators: Camilo Libedinsky

Areas: Computational neuroscience; Neural decoding; Neural interfaces

Libedinsky’s NeuroAI lab investigates the algorithms supporting attention, working memory, and decisions. It records individual neurons across macaque brain regions during cognitive tasks and relates those measurements to computational models and potential brain–machine interfaces. [1](https://nusgs.nus.edu.sg/thesis-advisors/psycdl) [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/)

A central problem is separating information that should be maintained from information that should update a memory. The lab’s tasks deliberately manipulate targets and distractors so that neural activity can be interpreted in relation to a specific cognitive operation, rather than a broad label such as “attention.” [1](https://nusgs.nus.edu.sg/thesis-advisors/psycdl) [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/)

**Methods:** Macaque single-neuron electrophysiology; Working-memory and distractor tasks; Population activity analysis; Computational models of cognition.

**Signals:** Prefrontal and prearcuate neuronal activity; Eye movements and task behavior; BCI control-related activity.

### Website review

Read the NUS research descriptions and the full PMC article, including methods; corrected the publication chronology.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [Working Memory Updating in the Macaque Lateral Prefrontal Cortex — issue publication](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/) — 2025-09-10.

- Specified two macaques and distinguished online publication from issue date.
- Avoided inconsistent current faculty-rank labels in the university directory.

- [NUS Graduate School: Camilo Libedinsky and NeuroAI](https://nusgs.nus.edu.sg/thesis-advisors/psycdl) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. NeuroAI aims, macaque recordings, network modeling and dated 2024 projects; inconsistent rank labels.
- [Working Memory Updating in the Macaque Lateral Prefrontal Cortex](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full article: task, two macaques, regional timing and population coding; online/issue dates.
- [NUS Life Sciences Institute research profile](https://www.nus.edu.sg/lsi/principal-investigators-3/camilo-libedinsky/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. NUS affiliation, research topics and the explicitly dated 2022 BCI-learning publication.

### Projects

#### Updating versus protecting working memory

*Published animal research: online 2024; issue 2025*

The published study recorded lateral-prefrontal and prearcuate single-neuron activity in two male macaques during a memory-updating task with distractors. Prearcuate updating appeared earlier than lateral-prefrontal updating, and target/distractor information occupied separable population subspaces. The article appeared online in December 2024 and in a September 2025 issue. [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/)

#### Learning in a BCI

*Published interface-learning research*

The NUS research profile lists a 2022 Journal of Neural Engineering study of neural correlates of learning in a linear-discriminant-analysis BCI paradigm. This connects the lab’s interest in adaptive neural representations to an explicit decoding task. [3](https://www.nus.edu.sg/lsi/principal-investigators-3/camilo-libedinsky/)

### Progress to track: Task-specific neural representations

The relevant scale is simultaneous neural-population information across cognitive conditions. No consistent current channel count or chronic implant lifetime is supplied by the inspected overview. [1](https://nusgs.nus.edu.sg/thesis-advisors/psycdl) [2](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12424936/)

### Study context

The faculty pages support the NUS affiliation but contain inconsistent rank fields, so no current rank is asserted. Cognitive-task results in macaques and the listed 2022 BCI-learning study do not establish a human memory-restoration device. [1](https://nusgs.nus.edu.sg/thesis-advisors/psycdl) [3](https://www.nus.edu.sg/lsi/principal-investigators-3/camilo-libedinsky/)

### Editorial notebook question

Can a decoder distinguish intentional memory updating from distraction when the same neurons participate in both?

## Laboratory for Advanced Neuroengineering and Translational Medicine (LANTRN)

**New York University Abu Dhabi · United Arab Emirates**

Investigators: Khalil Ramadi

Areas: Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation; Bioelectronics

LANTRN develops minimally invasive ways to study and modulate neural systems throughout the body. Its engineering toolkit includes microdevices, materials, nanoparticles, and electroceuticals, with gastrointestinal applications showing how neuroengineering extends beyond the brain and spinal cord. [1](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/research/faculty-labs-and-projects/laboratory-for-advanced-neuroengineering-and-translational-medic.html) [2](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html)

The laboratory connects device fabrication and wireless operation to biological access. A swallowed device can create an interface inside the digestive tract, but delivery of energy or information must still be matched to the particular cells and physiological process being studied. [1](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/research/faculty-labs-and-projects/laboratory-for-advanced-neuroengineering-and-translational-medic.html) [2](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html)

**Methods:** Ingestible device fabrication; Wireless inductive power; Optogenetic stimulation; Magnetic localization; Miniature sensors and wireless telemetry.

**Signals:** Gut neural responses to light; Capsule magnetic-field measurements; Gastrointestinal location and movement.

### Website review

Read the laboratory roster and both detailed university device reports, including the 2025 light capsule update.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [NYUAD researchers develop wireless light pill](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html) — 2025-09-02.

- Made animal validation and the 2023/2025 report dates explicit.
- Kept optogenetic sensitization separate from merely swallowing an LED capsule.

- [NYUAD LANTRN research profile](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/research/faculty-labs-and-projects/laboratory-for-advanced-neuroengineering-and-translational-medic.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Ramadi leadership and mechanical/electrical/materials approach to body-wide neuromodulation.
- [Wireless light pill to control gut neurons](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2025 ICOPS, LED capsule, induction power, 3D fabrication and freely moving animal use.
- [Smart pills for gastrointestinal disorders](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2023/february/nyuad-researcher-and-colleagues-develop-novel-smart-pills-that-improve-the-diagnosis-and-treatment-of-gastrointestinal-disorders.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2023 magnetic localization, wireless transmission and large-animal GI validation.

### Projects

#### ICOPS wireless light capsule

*Preclinical device report, 2025-09-02*

A 2025 university report describes an LED capsule powered by magnetic induction and fabricated with 3D printing. It is designed for optogenetic experiments, which require the relevant neurons to be made light-sensitive; the source describes operation in freely moving animals. [2](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html)

#### Tracking gastrointestinal motion

*Large-animal device research report, 2023-02-14*

The 2023 university report describes a swallowed sensor that measures external magnetic-field gradients and transmits measurements for three-dimensional localization. Validation was in large-animal gastrointestinal systems. It tracks capsule movement, rather than recording neural spikes. [3](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2023/february/nyuad-researcher-and-colleagues-develop-novel-smart-pills-that-improve-the-diagnosis-and-treatment-of-gastrointestinal-disorders.html)

### Progress to track: Access, localization, and untethered operation

The reviewed examples emphasize reaching the gut without implanted optical fibers and locating an ingestible sensor. No common electrode-count measure applies to these optical and tracking devices. [2](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html) [3](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2023/february/nyuad-researcher-and-colleagues-develop-novel-smart-pills-that-improve-the-diagnosis-and-treatment-of-gastrointestinal-disorders.html)

### Study context

Digestive, metabolic, and neurological conditions motivate the work. The light-capsule result is an experimental optogenetic platform, not evidence that swallowing it alone treats a human disorder. [1](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/research/faculty-labs-and-projects/laboratory-for-advanced-neuroengineering-and-translational-medic.html) [2](https://nyuad.nyu.edu/en/news/latest-news/science-and-technology/2025/september/nyuad-researchers-develop-wireless-light-pill.html)

### Editorial notebook question

Can an ingestible interface identify and perturb the relevant gut circuit with sufficient anatomical and cell-type precision?

## Jackson Laboratory

**Newcastle University · United Kingdom**

Investigators: Andrew Jackson

Areas: Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation; Rehabilitation & prosthetics

Jackson’s research connects movement neuroscience with implantable interfaces, epilepsy and sleep. The programme explores how brain signals can guide stimulation below an injury and how abnormal cortical dynamics might be controlled. It includes engineering development, animal experiments and clinically connected rehabilitation research. [1](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html)

The work spans neural recordings, spinal stimulation and optogenetic control. Non-invasive studies use EEG, EMG, functional electrical stimulation and movement measurements. Keeping these modalities separate matters: a human rehabilitation experiment does not itself establish readiness of an implant or optogenetic therapy. [1](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html)

**Methods:** Brain-controlled spinal stimulation; Optogenetic cortical control; EEG and EMG recording; Functional electrical stimulation; Kinematic analysis.

**Signals:** Cortical electrical activity; EEG; EMG; Movement kinematics; Sleep electrophysiology.

### Website review

Read staff research/publication content, the lab project page, the university medical-device page and news archive. A 2024–2026 search did not establish a newer project milestone.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

- Qualified CANDO as development and the cited 2023 work as nonhuman-primate research.
- Flagged the stale 2018 lab news archive instead of treating all current-work wording as a current trial status.

- [Andrew Jackson: Professor of Neural Interfaces](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Current institutional affiliation, program directions and 2023 closed-loop optogenetics publication listing.

- [Medical Devices research](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/biomedical-engineering/our-research/medical-devices/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. University context for CANDO; explicitly a project that explored a seizure-control implant.
- [Jackson Lab — News & Events](https://research.ncl.ac.uk/jacksonlab/newsevents/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Archive newest visible item dated 2018-05-09; no current clinical outcome.

### Projects

#### Brain-to-spinal hand-function prosthesis

*Implant and surgical-method development*

The university profile describes implantable electronics and surgical approaches for spinal stimulation controlled in real time by brain recordings. The intended application is restoration of hand function after spinal cord injury or stroke; the profile does not supply a clinical efficacy claim. [1](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html)

#### CANDO and rehabilitation studies

*Preclinical translation; separate human rehabilitation research*

CANDO pursued a cortical optogenetic implant for seizure control and a path toward human studies. Separately, the lab describes non-invasive upper-limb rehabilitation investigations in stroke and SCI using EEG, FES and movement measurements. CANDO’s stated trial ambition is not a verified trial outcome. [2](https://research.ncl.ac.uk/jacksonlab/projects/) [3](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/biomedical-engineering/our-research/medical-devices/)

### Progress to track: Closed-loop control and durable recording

Useful measures include the reliability of linking recorded signals to stimulation and longitudinal recording stability. The inspected pages do not establish a comparable channel-count trajectory. [1](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html) [2](https://research.ncl.ac.uk/jacksonlab/projects/) [3](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/biomedical-engineering/our-research/medical-devices/)

### Study context

The university profile documents Andrew Jackson’s neural-interface program. CANDO is described as a development project; the university’s listed 2023 study concerns closed-loop optogenetic control in nonhuman primates. The lab news archive ends in 2018, so it cannot establish a current human-trial milestone. [1](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/medical-sciences/people/profile/andrewjackson.html) [3](https://www.ncl.ac.uk/biomedical-engineering/our-research/medical-devices/) [4](https://research.ncl.ac.uk/jacksonlab/newsevents/)

### Editorial notebook question

Editorial question: How can stimulation reinforce useful motor plasticity without making control depend on fragile or drifting neural features?

## Rogers Research Group — Bio-Integrated Electronics

**Northwestern University · United States**

Investigators: John Rogers

Areas: Bioelectronics; Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation

John Rogers’ group develops soft, miniaturized electronics and microfluidic systems that can operate on or within living tissue. Its neurotechnology work belongs to a broader bioelectronics program spanning wireless devices, optical stimulation, physiological monitoring, and transient materials that dissolve after their useful period. [1](https://www.rogersgroup.northwestern.edu/research/biointegrated.php) [3](https://bioelectronics.northwestern.edu/about/)

The engineering strategy combines compliant structures, biocompatible materials, microfabrication, and carefully controlled energy or fluid delivery. Instead of asking only how many signals can be recorded, these systems ask how to deliver a local intervention with less mechanical burden and, in some designs, avoid a second removal procedure. [1](https://www.rogersgroup.northwestern.edu/research/biointegrated.php) [3](https://bioelectronics.northwestern.edu/about/)

**Methods:** Soft and bioresorbable materials; Microfluidic evaporative cooling; Wireless implantable optoelectronics; Thermal feedback and microsystem integration.

**Signals:** Nerve conduction; Local temperature; Optogenetically driven neural responses; Physiological and behavioral outcomes.

### Website review

Read group and institute descriptions, the 2022 cooling report, and the 2025/2026 optical-neuromodulation paper abstract.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [Patterned wireless transcranial optogenetics generates artificial perception — online publication](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-02127-6) — 2025-12-08.

- Added a recent, directly relevant neural-interface study with animal scope.
- Replaced an inaccessible PubMed record with readable evidence for the retained cooling claim.

- [Rogers Group: bio-integrated electronics research](https://www.rogersgroup.northwestern.edu/research/biointegrated.php) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Bio-integrated electronics program and implantable optical tools.
- [Dissolving implantable device relieves pain without drugs](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2022/06/dissolving-implant-relieves-pain-without-drugs) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2022 microfluidic evaporative nerve cooling, feedback, bioresorption and animal validation.
- [Querrey Simpson Institute for Bioelectronics: institutional leadership and scope](https://bioelectronics.northwestern.edu/about/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. QSIB organization and Rogers affiliation.
- [Patterned wireless transcranial optogenetics generates artificial perception](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-02127-6) — Abstract / publication record read; checked 2026-09-15. Published date, wireless cortical patterning, mouse cue discrimination and characterization; full article subscription-restricted.

### Projects

#### Bioresorbable nerve coolers

*Preclinical cooling device, 2022*

The 2022 project used a soft microfluidic device around a peripheral nerve to deliver localized evaporative cooling with temperature feedback. Reversible conduction block is a thermal intervention, distinct from electrically stimulating a nerve or releasing an analgesic. [2](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2022/06/dissolving-implant-relieves-pain-without-drugs)

#### Patterned wireless optogenetic cues

*Animal research; online 2025, journal issue 2026*

A study published online in December 2025 used an implanted wireless transcranial light stimulator to deliver spatial and temporal cortical patterns in mice. Cue-discrimination behavior, electrophysiology and optical/thermal characterization supported the experiment; this was not a human sensory-restoration trial. [4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-02127-6)

### Progress to track: Mechanical compatibility and controlled intervention

Meaningful scale includes device footprint, spatial selectivity, feedback control, and useful operating lifetime. A device’s ability to dissolve is a design feature with its own timing and materials constraints, not evidence of long-term therapeutic effectiveness. [1](https://www.rogersgroup.northwestern.edu/research/biointegrated.php) [4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-02127-6)

### Study context

The coolers are research devices, with surgical pain management discussed as a possible application. The broader group’s clinical bioelectronics activities should not be used to imply that every neurotechnology prototype has reached human clinical use. [1](https://www.rogersgroup.northwestern.edu/research/biointegrated.php) [2](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2022/06/dissolving-implant-relieves-pain-without-drugs)

### Editorial notebook question

Can a transient device deliver a predictable therapeutic dose throughout healing while its material properties and tissue contact change?

## Slutzky Neuroprosthetics Laboratory

**Northwestern University · United States**

Investigators: Marc Slutzky

Areas: Rehabilitation & prosthetics

Marc Slutzky’s Northwestern program develops myoelectric interfaces for rehabilitation. The latest inspected trial uses surface muscle activity to control games that train independent activation of muscles abnormally coupled after stroke. [1](https://www.feinberg.northwestern.edu/faculty-profiles/az/profile.html?xid=16463) [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full)

MINT maps two or three muscle signals to separate cursor directions. Reaching selected targets requires decoupling those muscles. The 2026 study compared this strategy with a one-muscle sham game and separated training adherence, movement kinematics and clinical arm-activity outcomes. [1](https://www.feinberg.northwestern.edu/faculty-profiles/az/profile.html?xid=16463) [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full)

**Methods:** Wearable surface EMG; Myoelectric game control; Home-based motor training; Randomized sham-controlled evaluation; Reaching kinematics.

**Signals:** Surface EMG envelopes; Arm movement; Wolf Motor Function Test; Training adherence.

### Website review

The lab site was unavailable; replaced its unsupported current-project claims with a live faculty page, full 2026 trial paper and university report.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [MINT randomized controlled trial](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full) — 2026-06-08.

- Replaced the 2021 feasibility emphasis with the 2026 controlled trial.
- Corrected news shorthand: 94 randomized, 59 completed/analyzed.
- Removed unverified current sleep, cooling and speech-project details; retained only freshly read evidence.
- Pruned inherited neural-decoding, neural-interface, neuromodulation and neuroimaging tags: the freshly substantiated profile now focuses on surface-EMG rehabilitation rather than a demonstrated neural decoder or stimulation/imaging program.

- [Marc W Slutzky — Northwestern faculty profile](https://www.feinberg.northwestern.edu/faculty-profiles/az/profile.html?xid=16463) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Current institutional position and lab identity.
- [Wearable Myoelectric Interface for Neurorehabilitation to Recover Arm Activity After Stroke: A Randomized Controlled Trial](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full methods/results, randomization and attrition, sham comparator, primary endpoint and per-protocol limitation.
- [Video game stroke rehab restores arm movement in chronic stroke survivors](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2026/06/video-game-stroke-rehab-restores-arm-movement-in-chronic-stroke-survivors) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. University context and clearly prospective device/lower-limb development.
- [Slutzky Lab research](https://sites.northwestern.edu/slutzkylab/research/) — Page unavailable; checked 2026-09-15. Direct and web retrieval failed; no retained claim depends on this page.

### Projects

#### MINT randomized trial

*Human trial, 2026*

The June 2026 paper reports 94 randomized participants; 59 completed six weeks and entered the modified intention-to-treat analysis. Training targeted 90 minutes a day, six days a week. Attrition and the difference between randomized and analyzed participants matter when interpreting feasibility. [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full)

#### Decoupling muscles and functional transfer

*Controlled rehabilitation outcomes, 2026*

The three-muscle group showed the clearest week-six result. Its advantage over sham on the timed Wolf test was significant in the per-protocol analysis; other secondary outcomes did not show clinically important changes. The study also measured reaching and follow-up at week ten. [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full)

### Progress to track: Adherence and functional improvement

Training dose, retained participants, muscle independence and transfer to arm activity are distinct outcomes. The full paper gives stronger interpretive detail than the news headline’s relative improvement multipliers. [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full) [3](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2026/06/video-game-stroke-rehab-restores-arm-movement-in-chronic-stroke-survivors)

### Study context

This is human rehabilitation evidence, with important attrition and analysis-population limits. Wireless device refinements and lower-limb applications are future work in the university report, not completed outcomes. [2](https://journals.sagepub.com/eprint/SCSQ2BHMR2SJKUV6TUGS/full) [3](https://news.northwestern.edu/stories/2026/06/video-game-stroke-rehab-restores-arm-movement-in-chronic-stroke-survivors)

### Editorial notebook question

Does improved muscle independence during home training persist after feedback is removed and transfer to useful reaching?

## Neural Computation Unit

**Okinawa Institute of Science and Technology · Japan**

Investigators: Kenji Doya

Areas: Computational neuroscience; Neural decoding

Doya’s unit asks how brains learn and choose actions under uncertainty. It combines reinforcement learning and Bayesian inference with experiments on neuromodulators and adaptive robots, offering a route from biological mechanisms to algorithms that can be tested in embodied behavior. [1](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu) [2](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu/research)

The research organization explicitly spans theoretical models, rodent systems neurobiology, and robotics. Dopamine and serotonin are investigated as components of learning and decision-making, while computational projects ask how hidden neural states and model parameters can be inferred from measurements. [1](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu) [2](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu/research)

**Methods:** Reinforcement-learning models; Bayesian state and parameter estimation; Rodent behavioral neuroscience; Adaptive robotics; Neuroscience workflow software.

**Signals:** Neural and behavioral measurements; Neuromodulator-related activity; Robot sensor and action data.

### Website review

Read current unit/research pages and the August 2026 research update.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [How to decipher smells like a fruit fly](https://www.oist.jp/news-center/news/2026/8/24/how-decipher-smells-fruit-fly) — 2026-08-24.

- Added Spi-Fly with the named collaborating researcher and benchmark limits.
- Retained Digital Brain and Fugaku dates as project periods, not completed whole-brain achievements.

- [OIST Neural Computation Unit](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Doya leadership, active projects including 2026 optical computing, tools and current unit news.
- [Neural Computation Unit research groups](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu/research) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Bayesian modeling, rodent neuromodulator experiments and adaptive robot research.
- [How to decipher smells like a fruit fly](https://www.oist.jp/news-center/news/2026/8/24/how-decipher-smells-fruit-fly) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Spi-Fly architecture, experimental odor datasets, few-shot/continual learning and limitations.

### Projects

#### Neuromodulators and decisions

*Experimental and theoretical research program*

The systems-neurobiology program studies how dopamine and serotonin participate in learning and choice. The important link is between a proposed computational role and measured behavior, rather than simply correlating one brain region with one task. [2](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu/research)

#### Digital brains and reproducible analysis

*Listed collaborative research and software programs*

The unit lists Brain/MINDS 2 Digital Brain and whole-brain data-assimilation projects, together with OptiNiSt and Neuro-Workflow analysis tools. These projects connect large models to experimental data and repeatable analysis pipelines. [1](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu)

#### Spi-Fly olfactory classification

*Computational research, 2026*

A 2026 collaboration involving unit researcher Kevin Max tested a sparse, three-layer spiking model on two odor datasets. The report highlights few-shot and continual learning while acknowledging stronger conventional classifiers overall and untested mixed-odor conditions. This is a simplified model, not a simulated complete fly brain. [3](https://www.oist.jp/news-center/news/2026/8/24/how-decipher-smells-fruit-fly)

### Progress to track: Models, behavior, and reproducibility

The current unit page lists Digital Brain for 2024–2030 and Fugaku whole-brain data assimilation for 2023–2026. These are project periods, not evidence that a complete human brain simulation has been achieved. [1](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu)

### Study context

The primary outputs are explanatory models, analysis tools, and adaptive systems. Relevance to neuroengineering comes from better interpretation of neural data and more capable adaptive controllers. [1](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu) [2](https://www.oist.jp/research/research-units/ncu/research)

### Editorial notebook question

Which learning rule explains both measured neural dynamics and behavior when the environment changes unexpectedly?

## Laboratory for Noninvasive Neuroengineering

**Rice University · United States**

Investigators: Jerzy Szablowski

Areas: Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation

Combines molecular engineering, focused ultrasound, and synthetic biology to access and monitor brain circuits without a permanent intracranial electrical interface. [1](https://www.szablowskilab.org/)

The group engineers ways to deliver genes or drugs to selected locations and to report molecular states through blood sampling. This expands the meaning of a neural interface beyond an electrode or optical camera. [1](https://www.szablowskilab.org/)

**Methods:** Focused ultrasound; AAV vector engineering; Chemogenetics; Synthetic serum reporters.

**Signals:** Gene expression; Synthetic serum marker concentration; Circuit responses.

### Website review

Read current lab news, both project pages and the full 2026 INTACT paper.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [INTACT publication announcement on lab news](https://www.szablowskilab.org/) — 2026-05-28.

- Replaced the generic serum-marker description with the 2026 INTACT result.
- Specified mouse/culture scope and three reporter readouts; kept molecular and electrical units distinct.
- Replaced the inherited Bioelectronics tag with Neural interfaces: the reviewed mechanisms are molecular reporting, ultrasound-assisted delivery and chemogenetics, not an electrical biointerface.

- [Szablowski Lab — research overview](https://www.szablowskilab.org/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Current platform directions and lab news through May 2026, including large-animal and INTACT updates.
- [Acoustically targeted chemogenetics program](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-neuromodulation) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. ATAC project mechanism and its dated 2018 landmark.
- [Noninvasive monitoring and reporter publications](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-monitoring-of-the-brain) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Earlier 2024 molecular-monitoring references, not a current in-press assertion.
- [Monitoring in vivo transcription with synthetic serum markers](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-73486-2) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full article: RNA sensor mechanism, mouse/culture validation, c-Fos/Arc and triplex reporter proof of concept.

### Projects

#### Acoustically targeted chemogenetics

*Preclinical molecular neuroengineering*

ATAC combines ultrasound targeting with gene delivery so that selected cells can subsequently respond to a chemogenetic actuator. The goal is spatial and cell-type selectivity without implanted intracranial hardware. [2](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-neuromodulation)

#### INTACT transcription readout

*Preclinical molecular reporting, 2026*

The May 2026 paper combines RNA sensors with released serum reporters. It measures selected transcript histories in mouse brain and cultured cells, including c-Fos and Arc, and demonstrates three reporter readouts in one animal. These are molecular measurements following gene delivery, not simultaneous single-neuron spike recordings. [4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-73486-2)

#### Vector and delivery design

*Research program*

The lab describes viral-vector engineering and focused-ultrasound approaches for access across the blood–brain barrier. Delivery efficiency and the ability to repeat interventions are part of the platform-development problem. [1](https://www.szablowskilab.org/)

### Progress to track: Molecular specificity and temporal resolution

Scale concerns molecular specificity, distinguishable reporters, delivery coverage and reporting dynamics. The 2026 experiment demonstrates triplex molecular readout; it does not establish thousands of independent electrical channels or millisecond temporal resolution. [4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-73486-2)

### Study context

“Noninvasive” here describes access strategies, not the absence of biological intervention: these programs can involve gene delivery and pharmacological control. The cited research does not establish a routine blood test for decoding human thoughts. [1](https://www.szablowskilab.org/) [2](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-neuromodulation) [4](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-73486-2)

### Editorial notebook question

How many distinct molecular processes can be measured independently, and how does reporter transport blur their timing?

### Sources

1. [Szablowski Lab — research overview](https://www.szablowskilab.org/) — Lab; publication date not recorded. Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Current platform directions and lab news through May 2026, including large-animal and INTACT updates.
2. [Acoustically targeted chemogenetics program](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-neuromodulation) — Lab; publication date not recorded. Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. ATAC project mechanism and its dated 2018 landmark.
3. [Noninvasive monitoring and reporter publications](https://www.szablowskilab.org/noninvasive-monitoring-of-the-brain) — Lab; publication date not recorded. Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Earlier 2024 molecular-monitoring references, not a current in-press assertion.
4. [Monitoring in vivo transcription with synthetic serum markers](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-73486-2) — Paper; 2026-05-27. Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full article: RNA sensor mechanism, mouse/culture validation, c-Fos/Arc and triplex reporter proof of concept.

## Luan Laboratory of Multimodal Bi-directional Neural Interface

**Rice University · United States**

Investigators: Lan Luan

Areas: Neural interfaces; Neuromodulation; Neuroimaging

Combines ultraflexible electrodes and optical measurements to study how neural populations change, recover after injury, and respond to precise stimulation. [1](https://www.luanlab.net/research-projects) [5](https://www.luanlab.net/)

The lab treats tissue integration as part of the measurement problem: a probe must keep useful contact with neurons while the tissue moves or reorganizes. Its research joins electrical recording, cellular imaging, and interventions across brain and spinal circuits. [1](https://www.luanlab.net/research-projects) [5](https://www.luanlab.net/)

**Methods:** Ultraflexible NET electrodes; Intracortical microstimulation; Two-photon imaging; Longitudinal population recording.

**Signals:** Extracellular spikes; Local neural activity; Vascular and inflammatory responses.

### Website review

Read the current lab identity, project and publication pages, two detailed university reports and the final 2026 platform paper.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [High-channel-count neural recording and stimulation platform with 5376 simultaneous recording channels](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8) — 2026-08-03.

- Corrected the laboratory name to its current home-page wording.
- Added the August 2026 final paper and kept rat surface validation separate from chronic single-neuron work.

- [Luan Lab — research projects](https://www.luanlab.net/research-projects) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Stimulation, stroke/plasticity and brain/spinal recording programs.
- [Rice — flexible nanoelectrodes and intracortical microstimulation](https://news.rice.edu/news/2023/flexible-nanoelectrodes-can-provide-fine-grained-brain-stimulation) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2023 mouse microstimulation experiment and intended sensory-prosthesis application.
- [Rice — spinalNET in freely moving mice](https://news.rice.edu/news/2024/rice-engineers-probe-could-help-advance-treatment-spinal-cord-disease-injury) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2024 freely moving mouse spinalNET study.
- [5,376-channel acquisition platform](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full article, final publication date, simultaneous channels, sampling rate and acute rat validation.
- [Luan Laboratory — home and research scope](https://www.luanlab.net/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Current Rice affiliation, laboratory name and multimodal scope.
- [Luan Laboratory — publications](https://www.luanlab.net/publications-1) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2025–2026 publication listing; accepted/preprint labels not treated as final outcomes.

### Projects

#### StimNET: selective, repeated activation

*Mouse stimulation experiments, 2023*

Ultraflexible intracortical electrodes were studied for low-threshold, spatially focused stimulation in mice. The work varies stimulus timing and amplitude while monitoring nearby neuronal responses; the intended application is more precise sensory prostheses. [2](https://news.rice.edu/news/2023/flexible-nanoelectrodes-can-provide-fine-grained-brain-stimulation)

#### spinalNET during natural movement

*Mouse motor behavior, 2024*

A joint Luan–Xie and Salk study used compliant electrodes to record mouse spinal neurons during motor behavior. The aim is to retain recordings in tissue that deforms with locomotion and to relate spinal population activity to movement. [3](https://news.rice.edu/news/2024/rice-engineers-probe-could-help-advance-treatment-spinal-cord-disease-injury)

#### Stroke and plasticity

*Research program*

The lab studies the relationship between neural function, blood vessels, inflammation, and recovery following injury, combining monitoring with open- and closed-loop modulation. This asks whether restored blood flow and restored circuit function follow the same trajectory. [1](https://www.luanlab.net/research-projects)

#### 5,376-channel cortical surface platform

*Acute rat micro-ECoG, 2026*

The August 2026 paper integrates acquisition electronics with flexible micro-ECoG arrays and demonstrates 5,376 concurrent recording channels at 20 kS/s in acute rat cortical recordings. This surface-field-potential result is distinct from chronic penetrating NET recordings. [4](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8)

### Progress to track: Stability × selectivity × coverage

Read the NET progression by configuration and biological setting. The separately documented 5,376-channel platform is acute rat surface micro-ECoG, not 5,376 chronically tracked single neurons. Stimulation selectivity and longitudinal recording stability need their own measurements. [1](https://www.luanlab.net/research-projects) [4](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8)

### Study context

The cited stimulation and spinal recording studies are preclinical. Visual restoration is a research direction; these sources do not establish a clinically available visual prosthesis. [2](https://news.rice.edu/news/2023/flexible-nanoelectrodes-can-provide-fine-grained-brain-stimulation) [3](https://news.rice.edu/news/2024/rice-engineers-probe-could-help-advance-treatment-spinal-cord-disease-injury)

### Editorial notebook question

If more contacts are added, does the number of independently controllable neural populations rise without sacrificing long-term stability?

## Mechatronics and Haptic Interfaces Lab

**Rice University · United States**

Investigators: Marcia O’Malley

Areas: Rehabilitation & prosthetics

Develops robots, wearable sensing, and tactile feedback to measure and train human sensorimotor performance. [1](https://mahilab.rice.edu/)

MAHI joins dynamic systems and control with human movement experiments. The important output is how people move, learn, and use their limbs, measured through the interaction between a person and an engineered device. [1](https://mahilab.rice.edu/)

**Methods:** Robotic movement assessment; Hand and wrist sensing; Vibrotactile feedback; Motor-learning experiments.

**Signals:** Joint motion; Force and movement dynamics; Task performance.

### Website review

Read the lab home, both project descriptions, publication list through 2025 and the full wrist-assessment paper.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

- Added the latest listed wrist study, specifying eight intact participants and simulated impairment.
- Retained the distinction between motor-task performance and clinical rehabilitation benefit.

- [MAHI Lab — scope and research](https://mahilab.rice.edu/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. O’Malley-led mechatronics/haptics program and 2025 home news.
- [Human motion assessment for rehabilitation](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/human-motion-assessment-rehabilitation) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Wearable motion assessment, capacity versus daily performance and proposed clinical workflow.
- [Mirror tracing and real-time haptic feedback](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/mirror-tracing-proxy-endovascular-surgery) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Three-axis mirror tracing, vibrotactile feedback and retention protocol.

- [Personalized Range of Motion Scaled Wrist Pointing for Robotic-Based Motor Impairment Assessment](https://mahilab.rice.edu/sites/default/files/publications/Rice%202025ICORR.pdf) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full paper: personalized target scaling, eight participants, brace restriction and assessment outcomes.

### Projects

#### Rehabilitation outside the clinic

*Rehabilitation technology research*

The motion-assessment program develops lightweight hardware to track hand and wrist use and quantify movement. It distinguishes the ability to perform a clinic task from performance in everyday activities, aiming to inform therapist decisions. [2](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/human-motion-assessment-rehabilitation)

#### Learning with touch feedback

*Human motor-learning experiments*

A mirror-tracing experiment uses a three-degree-of-freedom manipulator and a wearable vibrating bracelet. Feedback derived from speed or accuracy lets investigators study training strategies and retention after feedback is removed. [3](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/mirror-tracing-proxy-endovascular-surgery)

#### Personalized wrist assessment

*Human engineering experiment, 2025*

The 2025 ICORR study used OpenWrist in eight neurologically intact participants, with a brace simulating restricted movement. Scaling targets to measured wrist range improved target reachability and reduced the confound of unreachable goals in movement assessment. It was not a stroke-patient efficacy trial. [5](https://mahilab.rice.edu/sites/default/files/publications/Rice%202025ICORR.pdf)

### Progress to track: Functional movement and retained learning

Useful measures include motion quality, actual daily use, and whether a skill remains after cues disappear. More sensors alone do not establish greater rehabilitation benefit. [2](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/human-motion-assessment-rehabilitation) [3](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/mirror-tracing-proxy-endovascular-surgery)

### Study context

The mirror task models elements of tool navigation; performance gains in that task do not by themselves establish improved surgical outcomes. At-home sensing is described as a research goal, not a replacement for clinical assessment. [2](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/human-motion-assessment-rehabilitation) [3](https://mahilab.rice.edu/research/mirror-tracing-proxy-endovascular-surgery)

### Editorial notebook question

Which feedback improves long-term independent performance rather than just performance while the device is assisting?

## Nanoscale Neural Interface Laboratory

**Rice University · United States**

Investigators: Chong Xie

Areas: Neural interfaces; Bioelectronics

Engineers tissue-integrated neural electrodes and acquisition systems for dense, distributed, and longitudinal electrophysiology. [1](https://www.chongxie.net/projects)

The central engineering strategy is to reduce the probe’s size and mechanical mismatch with neural tissue. The group combines ultraflexible structures, fabrication, optical assessment of tissue response, and system integration for recordings that persist over time. [1](https://www.chongxie.net/projects)

**Methods:** Nanoelectronic threads; Electron-beam lithography; Two-photon tissue imaging; Multichannel acquisition.

**Signals:** Single-neuron extracellular activity; Surface micro-ECoG.

### Website review

Read every retained project page, current publication list, final 2026 acquisition paper and the 2025 temporal-coding paper abstract.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

- Confirmed the 2026 platform’s final August publication rather than relying on its March preprint listing.

- [High-density, large-scale neural recording](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/high-density,-large-scale-neural-recording) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Approximately micrometre-thick structures and distributed recording designs.
- [Scalable longitudinal electrophysiology systems](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/scalable-systems-for-high-throughput-and-longitudinal-electrophysiology-in-clinical-research.-) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. SpikeGadgets collaboration explicitly targets mouse/rat cohorts.
- [5,376-channel acquisition platform](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Full final paper: concurrent channel capacity and acute rat surface validation.

Male mice, 15 consecutive days, temporal-versus-rate coding comparison and stability limits.

### Projects

#### Tissue-integrated electrodes

*Preclinical longitudinal electrophysiology*

The group uses ultraflexible electrodes and in-vivo imaging to examine integration with cells and capillaries. The lab reports recordings that track individual neurons over months; biological integration and signal continuity are linked design goals. [2](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/tissue-integrated-neural-electrodes)

#### Distributed recording at scale

*Device and animal research*

The high-density program uses approximately 1 µm-thick structures and fabrication techniques intended to cover both local circuit volumes and distant brain regions. Device thinness is a physical dimension, not a recording-channel count. [3](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/high-density,-large-scale-neural-recording)

#### Acquisition and experimental throughput

*Preclinical systems; separate surface platform*

A described SpikeGadgets collaboration targets tools for long-term recordings in large mouse and rat cohorts. Separately, the 2026 Luan–Xie–Chi platform provides 5,376 simultaneous channels at 20 kS/s with acute rat cortical surface validation. [4](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/scalable-systems-for-high-throughput-and-longitudinal-electrophysiology-in-clinical-research.-) [5](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8)

#### Stable visual representations across days

*Longitudinal mouse electrophysiology, 2025*

The August 2025 study tracked visual cortical populations in male mice for 15 consecutive days using ultraflexible arrays. Fine temporal spike patterns yielded more stable stimulus representations than firing-rate coding in the tested conditions; this is a coding comparison, not a universal guarantee that every neuron remains stable. [7](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-025-62069-2)

### Progress to track: Sites, concurrent channels, and stable units

Increasing the fabricated site count, the simultaneously digitized channels, and the neurons tracked over months are different achievements. The surface platform belongs on a separate series from penetrating NET arrays. [2](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/tissue-integrated-neural-electrodes) [3](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/high-density,-large-scale-neural-recording) [5](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44385-026-00103-8)

### Study context

The acquisition project’s title mentions clinical research, but its stated development models are mice and rats. This profile does not reinterpret that title as a human implant demonstration. [4](https://www.chongxie.net/Projects/scalable-systems-for-high-throughput-and-longitudinal-electrophysiology-in-clinical-research.-)

### Editorial notebook question

Which limitation dominates next: insertion and packaging, concurrent readout, or maintaining the same useful neurons across months?

## Realtime Neural Engineering Laboratory

**Rice University · United States**

Investigators: Caleb Kemere

Areas: Neural decoding; Computational neuroscience; Neural interfaces

Builds real-time tools to investigate how hippocampal population activity supports learning, memory, and offline processing. [1](https://rnel.rice.edu/)

The lab combines recordings in behaving animals with online decoding and interventions. Detecting a neural event quickly enough to change it turns an observed correlation into a test of the event’s causal contribution. [1](https://rnel.rice.edu/)

**Methods:** Sharp-wave ripple detection; Closed-loop perturbation; Hidden Markov models; Gaussian-process latent-variable models; Open-source acquisition and analysis.

**Signals:** Hippocampal spikes; Local field potentials; Animal trajectories.

### Website review

Read lab scope, memory project, publications, the 2024 rat report, the collaborator’s publication page, and the MiniFAST abstract/introduction through a working PMC fallback.

Status: reviewed. Checked: 2026-09-15.

Newest dated source found: [MiniFAST: a sensitive and fast miniaturized microscope for in vivo neural recording](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12988765/) — 2026-03-14.

- Explicitly dated the ripple-system landmarks and online-decoder preprint.
- Added the March 2026 MiniFAST study, keeping freely behaving calcium imaging separate from head-fixed voltage imaging and windowed high-speed acquisition.

- [Kemere Lab — realtime neural engineering](https://rnel.rice.edu/) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. Realtime causal experiments, latent-variable models and open tools.
- [Memory and closed-loop research](https://rnel.rice.edu/research/memory.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2012 causal ripple work and 2019 open-source intervention system.
- [Rice — hippocampal representations during rest](https://news.rice.edu/news/2024/brain-rest-neurons-rehearse-future-experience) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. 2024 rat hippocampal study during rest; no direct access to subjective dream content.
- [Kemere Lab — publications and software studies](https://rnel.rice.edu/pubs.html) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. GhostiPy 2021 and RealtimeDecoder 2024 preprint listings.
- [Robinson Lab — collaborative publications including MiniFAST](https://www.robinsonlab.com/publications) — Page content read; checked 2026-09-15. MiniFAST March 2026 publication listing and Kemere coauthorship; no quantitative result retained.
- [MiniFAST: a sensitive and fast miniaturized microscope for in vivo neural recording](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12988765/) — Abstract / publication record read; checked 2026-09-15. Abstract and introduction read: advanced CMOS sensor, freely behaving mouse calcium imaging, in-vitro high-speed voltage imaging and head-fixed mouse voltage imaging.

### Projects

#### Closed-loop ripple experiments

*Animal systems neuroscience*

The project page connects a 2012 causal spatial-memory study with a 2019 open-source real-time sharp-wave-ripple disruption system. These are dated foundations for closed-loop experiments, not newly reported 2026 outcomes. [2](https://rnel.rice.edu/research/memory.html)

#### Latent structure during sleep

*Rat recordings and computational analysis*

Statistical decoding and latent-variable models seek structure in ensemble activity when overt behavior provides few labels. The 2024 Rice–Michigan work studied how rat hippocampal spatial representations change during post-experience rest. [3](https://news.rice.edu/news/2024/brain-rest-neurons-rehearse-future-experience)

#### Reusable experimental software

*Open-source research tools*

The lab publication page lists GhostiPy signal-processing software (2021) and RealtimeDecoder for online clusterless decoding as a 2024 preprint. These address analysis throughput and intervention timing; the page’s preprint label is preserved. [4](https://rnel.rice.edu/pubs.html)

#### MiniFAST imaging collaboration

*Animal and in-vitro imaging, 2026*

The March 2026 MiniFAST paper upgrades a one-photon miniscope with a more sensitive CMOS sensor. It demonstrates calcium imaging in freely behaving mice and voltage-indicator imaging in culture and head-fixed mice. The highest sensor frame rate uses a reduced image window, not full-resolution video. [6](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12988765/)

### Progress to track: Decoding latency and validated population structure

More simultaneous neurons are useful only if the system extracts reliable state estimates within the time needed for intervention. The lab’s sources support algorithms and causal experiments, not a single hardware-doubling series. [1](https://rnel.rice.edu/) [2](https://rnel.rice.edu/research/memory.html)

### Study context

These sources describe memory experiments and research tools. Aspirations concerning traumatic memories are not evidence of demonstrated selective memory treatment in people. [1](https://rnel.rice.edu/) [2](https://rnel.rice.edu/research/memory.html)

### Editorial notebook question

Can a real-time decoder distinguish the content of a replay event well enough to perturb one hypothesis while preserving matched control events?
